The emulsion test however, is a method to determine the presence of lipids using wet chemistry. This same method can test for lipids in food as well.\', \'Lipid profile or a lipid panel test, is a panel of blood tests that serves as an initial screening tool for abnormalities in lipids, such as cholesterol and triglycerides, as well as certain genetic diseases. The emulsion test is a method to determine the presence of lipids using wet chemistry. The procedure is for the sample to be suspended in ethanol, allowing lipids present to dissolve. The liquid, which is alcohol with dissolved fat, is then decanted into water. Since lipids do not dissolve in water, when the ethanol is diluted, it falls out of the solution to give a cloudy white emulsion.\']', 'short_answers': "[['lipid panel', 'Lipid profile'], ['Emulsion test'], ['Emulsion test'], ['Lipid panel test']]"}
    Based on the provided information, it appears that there are two methods to test for lipids:1. Lipid Profile: This is a panel of blood tests that measures the levels of various lipids, such as cholesterol and triglycerides, in the blood. It is used as an initial screening tool for abnormalities in lipids.2. Emulsion Test: This is a method to determine the presence of lipids using wet chemistry. The procedure involves suspending the sample in ethanol, allowing lipids to dissolve, and then decanting the liquid into water. Since lipids do not dissolve in water, the presence of lipids is indicated by a cloudy white emulsion.

The first epidemic was smallpox, followed by other diseases including a probable Typhus outbreak, influenza, diphtheria and measles all of which ravaged the Inca people. The downfall of the Incan Empire was primarily due to a combination of factors, including:1. **Smallpox epidemic**: The introduction of smallpox by the Spanish conquistadors had a devastating impact on the Incan population, weakening their defenses and creating an environment of chaos and disarray.2. **Civil War**: The internal conflict between the brothers Huáscar and Atahualpa, which began in 1529 and lasted until 1532, further weakened the empire and created opportunities for the Spanish to exploit.3. **Technological disadvantage**: The Inca army was poorly equipped to face the Spanish steel armor and firearms, which gave them a significant technological advantage.4. **Lack of horses**: The absence of horses in the Americas meant that the Inca did not develop tactics to fight cavalry, making them vulnerable to Spanish attacks.5. **Spanish conquest**: The arrival of Spanish conquistadors, led by Francisco Pizarro, who were well-equipped, well-trained, and determined to conquer the empire.6. **Disease and population decline**: The introduction of European diseases, such as smallpox, influenza, and measles, which decimated the Incan population and further weakened their ability to resist the Spanish.7. **Internal divisions and power struggles**: The Incan Empire was an amalgamation of different cultures and peoples, which created internal divisions and power struggles that weakened the empire's ability to resist the Spanish.These factors, combined with the external pressures of the Spanish conquest, ultimately led to the downfall of the Incan Empire.

The Seljuk Empire began to take shape in the 11th century, specifically around 950 when the apical ancestor of the Seljuqs, Seljuk, migrated to Khwarezm and converted to Islam. However, the empire as we know it today started to expand under Malik Shah I in the late 11th century.As for the end of the empire, it is difficult to pinpoint an exact date. However, the Seljuk Empire began to decline in the 12th century, and by the 13th century, it had fractured into smaller emirates, known as the Anatolian beyliks. The last of these emirates, the Ottoman Empire, would eventually rise to power and conquer the rest.The Seljuk Empire's decline can be attributed to various factors, including internal conflicts, the rise of the Khwarezmshah, and the Mongol invasions in the 13th century. By the 13th century, the Seljuk Empire had effectively collapsed, and its territories were absorbed into other empires, including the Mongol Empire and the Ottoman Empire.So, to answer your question, the Seljuk Empire began to take shape in the 11th century and ended in the 13th century, with the empire's territories being absorbed into other empires.
